ORDER 


New Delhi, the 30th October 1968 


S . O . 3905 . — Shri Davloor Ramkrishna was granted Custom Clearance Permit No . 
P / J /2364235 / N /MN / 28 / H / 27. 28 dated 23rd August, 1968 for Rs. 15 ,000 / - for import 
of his car . He has applied for a duplicate of custom clearance permit on the ground that 
the original custom clearance permit has been lost /misplaced . It is further stated that 
the original Custom Clearance permit was not registered with any Customs House and 
pot utilised . 


In support of this contention Shri Davloor Ramkrishna has filed an affidavit. I am 
satisfied that the original Custom clearance permit No. P / J / 2364235 has been lost or 
misplaced and direct that a duplicate Custom Clcarance Permit should be issued to him . 
The original custom clearance permit is cancelled . 


[No. 2(B-183 )/68.69 /BI. S/ 4029. ) 

P . C . NANDA , 
Deputy Chief Controller of Imports & Exports , 

for Chief Controller of Imports & Export .

10 , Issue 1. - - The dispute is between the workmen represented by different unions. Onc 
of the Unions namely , Madras Port & Doch Workers Congress , is in favour of the present 
practice of the management in the mattei of promotion of workers to the category of 
Tindals . The other Unions arc not in favour of the present practice. According to them , 
the regular promotion to the category of Tindals should be lhluw open dy v Mazdoors , 
who have been acting in casual vacancies over a long period as per clatisc 30 of the Madras 
Dock Workers ( Regulation of Employment) Scheme, 1956 . As the employees demands 
are not conceded with regard to the promotion to the category of Tindals , the dispute has 
been referred to this Tribunal for adjudication , 

11. According to the Dock Labour Board , the present scheme necils no modification in 
regard to promotions . The post of Winchman is a skilled one ind carries higher emolu 
ments than the post of reserve pool mazdoor . The Boord is treating the post of Reserve 
Mazdoor as lower post to the post of Winchman for the purposes of promotion to the post 
of Tindal for the last so many years. Only in the year 1957 few posts of Tindals were filled 
by promoting mazdoors as there was shortage of Winchuen , The i omal practice was 
and is to promote Winchinen as a Tindal and not reserve lazdoor. The posts of Tindal, 
Winchmen and Signalman are not separate or independent categories , as alleged by some 
of the Unions . 

12 . Tindals , Winchmen , Signalmen und Madoors we workmen registered under the 
Dock Workers (Regulation of Employment) Scheme. Clause 16 empowers the Board to 
Arrange for the chalssilication of korkers by categories in the registors The Dock Workers 
registered under the scheine are classified into (a ) Foreman (Serang ), (b ) Tindal, ( c ) 
Winchman ( 1 ) Steve on N . zdoor and ( t ! Tally Clerk Shc contention of the Board is 
the reserve pool 111. duor has do preluctif claim for th : purposes of promotion to the 
post of Tindals . Section 19 of the Dock Workers ( Regulation of Finployment ) Scheme 
provides that I vacancy , other than a casual vacancy , in any category of workers in a 
reserve pool register hall ocarily be filled by promotion of a worher from the next 
Jower category . 


13 The allegation that in Bombay or Clicutta Mazdoors le promoted in the insual as 
well as regular incios of lindil , in denied ly the Bord . According to the Madras 
Harbour Workers Union , there is a reserve pool register containing the lines of the 
registered workers , namely , Tinduts, Winchmen , Signalmen and Mazdoors. The stovedore 
working is done Ly workers in Ik which consists of one Tindal and 9 Mazdoors. A 
Mazdoor will be posted to the post of a Vinchman or Signalman only after he undergoes 
a training and obtains it certificate front the Duch Safety Inspector . Only if i Mazdoor 
15 qualified , he is fit for promotion to the post of Winchn or Signalman . According to 
the Union, it was the practice of the 11 . nacomert to promote only Muzdots to the post 
of Tindals whenever a regular vacancy arose . This was the correct procedure . But it was 
not followed subsequently . Only in 1966 a special category called " 9187man " was created 
Thc wages of these different catcgories of workers are fixed on the .1 of 111.: 14 - yee of a 
Mazdoor . The basic wages of a Mazloor 1re fixed at Rs. 4 - 12 per day, the Signalman 
gets 30 paise more than a Mazdoor; the Winchman gets 50 paise niore than the Mazdoor 
and the Tindal gets Re. 1 / 2 more than the Mazdoor. The management is now promoting 
only thc Winchmen to the post of Tindal in a l egular Vacancy and the seniormost Mazdoor 
in the shift to the post of a Tindal in a casual vacancy. As the management is now promot 
ing Winchmen only to the post of Tindal. many cniornost inazdoors have been denied 
their promotion There are about 52 mazloors now and thun promoted in casual vacancies 
of Tindals during the past 15 years . These persons have got better claim for being pro 
moted in the regul.tr vacancies of Tindals than most of the Winchmen now in service . As 
already stated , the management follows only the practice of promoting the Winchinen as 
Tindals Only in 1957 , some posts of Tindals have been filled up promoting Mazdoors, 
as there was a shortage of Winchmen . 


14 . The action of the Board under clause 19 was approved by the High Court in 
W . P . 1321 / 66 . In that case , ( W . P . 1321 /66 ) a mazdoor registered in the reserve pool prayod 
for issuc of a Wiit of Mandamus directing the Madras Dock Labour Board to refrain 
from promoting Winchinen and Mazdoors as Tindals and permanent Winchmen respectively 
and from registering casual Winchmen as permanent Winchmen by implementing resolution 
No. 4 passed in meeting No . II of 1965 -66 held on 21st March , 1966 . That retition was 
dismissed . The relevant clause in the Dock Workers (Regulation of Employment) Scheme. 
1956 are clauses 16 . 19 and 30 . The High Court negatived the contention that casual 
Winchmen should not be promoted as registered Winchmen . According to the High Court, 
even in respect of Stevedore Mazdoors, they cannot complain 19 according to clause 19 , 
only when there is promotion from a lower category , 19 ( 2 ) should be observed . Permanent 
vacancies should be filled up according to clause 19 , that is by promotion of a worker from 
the next category . In the course of the order , the High Court has observed , that " apart 
from the arrangement of categories in clause 16 , the Board has uniformly acted on the 
basis that Tindal is a higher category to Winchmen , and Winchmen higher to Stevedorc 
Mazdoor, Promotion have been made from Stevedore Mazdoor to Winchmen . In 1957 ,

1. Revision of existing leave facilities. 
( a ) Casual leaver - 12 days in a calendar year subject to the following conditions : 
( 1) Staff will be entitled to 12 days casual leave in a year to cover casual absence 

from duty . 
( ii ) Casual leave may be availed of in continuous period not exceeding three 

days at a time. 
(til) A Sunday or a holiday falling within or continuously with the period for 

which casual leave is taken will be counted as part of the casual leave 
taken , and tbe aggregate period of absence from duty hence should not 

exceed three working days at a timo. 
(iv ) Casual leavc will not be accumulated and any part of it remainiog unenjoyed 

at the end of the year will lapse . 
( h ) Sick leave — 12 days in a calendar year subject to the following conditions: 
( 1) Staff will be entitled to 12 days sick leave in a year to cover absence from 

duty on account of sickness . 
( il ) Sick leave may be availed of in continuous period not exceeding three day! 

at a time. 
( ii.) A Sunday or a holiday falling within or continuously with the period for 

which sick leave is taken will be counted as part of the sick leave taken , 
and the aggregate period of absence from duty hence should not exceed 

three working days at a time. 
( iv ) Sick leave will not be accumulated and any part of it remaining unenjoyed 

at the end of the year will lapse . 
( c ) Annual or privilege leave — 20 days for every one year of service with accumu 

lation up to sixty days subject to the following conditions ; 
(i) Staff will be entitled to 20 days privilege leave for every completed year of 

service . 
( a ) Privilege leave must be enjoyed and shall not be encashod; but in special 

circumstances in casc of Lower Grade Staff inembers one-half can be 
encashed and the other one-hall, shall be enjoyed (while going on 

privilege leave ). 
(ii) Privilege Icavo may be accumulated up to a maximum period of 60 days . 

All accumulated leave in excess of this will be considered as having lapsed 
and will be forfeited except when such excess accumulation was involun 
tary on the part of the staff and was caused by the written refusal of the 

leave by the Management. 
( l) For the purposes of calculeting the privilege leave due to staff , his probationary 

period of service, if any, will be taken into account. 
( iv ) When the member of the staff avails himself of privilege leave, he cannot 

attach thereto any casual leave due to him . 
(v ) A Sunday or holiday falling at the beginning of privilege leave period shall 

not be counted as part of the leave . In all other cases , i.e ., when a 
Sunday or holiday falls between the first and the last days of privilego 

leave period it /they shall be counted as part of the leave taken . 
(vi) An employce shall be eligible to encash the whole of privilege teave due to 

him and also proportionate leave accruing up to the happening of the 
contigencies below mentioned , subject to a maximum of forty days 

under any of the following circumstances : 
( a ) Death while in company s employment, 
(b ) Retirement on attaining age of retirement or after completion of THIRTY 

YEARS continuous service ; 
(c) Resignation on account of ill-health through no fault of the cmployee; 

( d ) Retrenchment. 
NOTE . A member of the Staff who is due to retire , or wishes to retire on com 

pletion of 30 years service, may, at his option , either cacash the leave 

remaining unenjoyed or may enjoy it even after retirement. 
(d ) Special Sick Leave 
(i) Staff will be entitled to the grant of Special Sick Leave only when thev do 

not have any leave to their credit , and at the rate shown hereunder

AWARD 
The Government of India in tho Ministry of Labour and Employment have by their 
Order No. 28 /70 /63-LRIV , dated 10th August , 1965 referred to this Tribunal an industrial 
disputo between the employers in relation to the Bombay Port Trust, Bombay and their 
workmen in respect of tho matter specified the following schedule : -- 

SCHEDULE 
(1 ) Whether the action of the management in treating Sbri Malvyya Kathmyyiu , 

Nawgani Wagon Repair Shop, Engineering Department, Bombay Port Trust, 
Bombay, senior to Shri Babulal Nohar, Holderman of the gamo Department 
for the purposes of officiating or promotion to the insolated post of Fitter 

( Sick Siding ) is justiflod ? 
(2 ) If not to what relief is Shri Babulal Nohar entitled ? 
2 . The Bombay Port Trust Railwaymen s Union has by its statements of claim contended 
that the workman Shri Babulul Nobur was first employed by the Bombay Port Trust as 
a mazdoor. He was senior to Shri Malayya who was also employed as mazdoor in the 
sick siding section , Shri Babulal was substantively promoted to the post of holderman on 
1st July , 1957, Even prior to this promotion ho used to be given acting chances in cave 
arrangements in higher capacities such as Boiler Maker , Rivetter , Assistant Fitter and 
Assistant Blacksmith otc ., and during the whole period he has been considered as seoior 
to Shri Malayya . However , in spite of this position about seniority the Bombay Port 
Trust promoted Shri Malayya as fitter for the first time from 12th June 1961 on somo 
Aimsy grounds. Shri Malayya has not even passed any test which was introduced for 
promotions and was not eligible. Shri Babula ! Nohar has been senior to Malayya on thc 
basis of his carlici Appointment as a mazdoor Promotions to the posts boiler maker , 
rivetter , assistant fitter , ſitter and blacksmith used to be given to the senior mazdoors and 
on the basis of this past practice Shri Babulal Nohar has always been given the first chance 
of promotion in leave vacancies in any of these positions. It has been further contended 
that Shri Babulal having given the test successfully when required to do so was selected 
for promotion against Shri Malayya and thus Babulal had established his superior claim 
for promotion and it was therefore unjust that Shri Babulal should be superseded subge 
quently . It is alleged that the Port Trust Administration has all along actcd wrongly in 
the case of Babulal under the inflence and pressure from the Bombay Port Trust Employees 
Uplon . The Union had referred the matter to the Regional Labour Commissioner (Central) 
Bombay but the Port Trust Administration had refused to recognise the seniority of 
Babulal. The appointment of Shri Malayya to the post of fitter in 1961 was virtually . In 
supersession of tho claim of Babulal and this Tribunal should set aside the order and diredi 
the Port Trust to appoint Babulal Mohar as a fitter . 

3 . The Trustees of the Port of Bombay have by their written statement contended that 
the dispute in this case being about a claim for the promotion of Shri Babulal Nohar in 
prefercnce to Shri Malayya Ratnayya this Tribunal has no jurisdiction to adjudicate the 
domand . They have admitted that Shri Babulal Nohar was appointed as a mazdoor about 
two years carlier than Malayya Ratnayya but have contended that Shri Babulal Nehar wag 
appointed as a mazdoor in the yard section while Shil Malayya was from the beginning 
working as a mazdoor in the sick giding section there being two separate sections known 
as the Yard and Sick Siding Section in the Wagon Repair Shop . They have admitted that 
Babulal Nohar was senior to Malayya Ratnayya but have contended that this seniority 
had no relevance in respect of the appointment for the post of ſitter. The post of fitter 
in the sick siding section was not in the established channel of promotion for mazdoon 
but it was an isolatert rost open to any person qualified subject to the standing rule of 
seniority - cum -suitability and according to the practice and the rules of the Bombay Port Trolet 
seniority was to be reckoned with reference to the date of first appointment to the particular 
category to which permanent appointments are under consideration , 

4 . They have alleged that the relative seniority between Babulal Nohar and Malayya 
Ratnavya depended on who of them had first acted in the said nogt. According to them 
Malayya Ratnayva having acted prior to Babulal Nohar acquired seniority over the latter 
for promotion to the post of fltter . He was promoted to the post of Assistant Fitter in 
1946 - thereafter as a boller maker from 1st Mav . 1946 to 11th July , 1046 and as Arter 
from 1st March , 1947 in 18th Trine. 1047 At that time Shri Bahulal Nohar was working 
In the yard section and Shri Malavya Ratnayva was senſor to him . As regards the test 
they have contended that the question of requiring Malayya Ratnayya to pass a test for 


SES 3 (1 ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : NOVEMBER 9 , 1968 /KARTIKA 18, 1890 4971 

- --- - - -- - - - 
promotion to the higher post did not arise as he had already acted as a fitter prior to 
tho passing of the said T . R . and had qualified for promotion to that post. They have 
alleged that the same rulo was made applicable to Babual Nohar since he too was not 
subjected to any departmental test according to the practice as well as the rules in force 
on the date on which the promotion was made. Shri Malayya Ratnayya was senioi to 
Babulal Nohar and there was no question of supersession and Babulal Nohar was not 
entitled to any reliet . 


5 . When the matter was fixed for hering Shri S . K . Shelye , General Secretary of the 
Bombay Port Trust Employees Union requested the Tribunal to add his union as a party 
to the procecdings contending that Shri Malayya Ratnayya was a member of the union 
and he should be permitted to put forth his say in the reference . He was permitted and 
the matter was again fixed for hearing on 14th August , 1968 . 

6 . But in the meantime the employers, the Bombay Port I rust, gave an application 
for amendment of thc written statement contending that the dispute eferred to his 
Tribunal by the reference order was only an individual dispute and not an industrial 
dispute . It was alleged that the members of the B . P . T . Railwayman s Union had not 
şupported the cause of the workman Shri Babulal Nohar . Neither the managing committee 
nor the general body had given a mandate to Shri Kale to raise the dispute on behalf of 
Shri Babulal Nohar and as it was an individual dispute the Tribunal had no jurisdiction 
and the same should be rejected After hearing the parties the amendment was allowed . 

7 . The same day the employers had given an application requesting the Tribunal to 
direct the B . P . T . Railwaymen s Union to produce ( 1 ) a certified copy of the constitution 
of the B .PL. Railwayinen s Uniun (2 ) Membership register of the Union for a period of 
one year prior to 10th August, - 1965 ( 3 ) A list of members of the managing committee 
of the union for a period of one year prior to 10th August , 1965 ( 4 ) Counterfoils of 
receipts (5 ) Minutes book maintained by the union regarding its proceedings for a period 
of one year prior to 10th August, 1965 and ( 6 ) a certified copy of the resolution passed 
either by the members of the Managing Committee or at the general body meeting of the 
union authorising the general secretary of the union to raise the industrial dispute , 

8 . As per this application thc union was directed to produce the documents in Court. 
However , Shri Kule the President of the B . P . T . Railwaymen s union sent an application 
for postponement of the hearing of the dispute on the ground of his illness. So again the 
dispute was adjourncd und fixed for hearing on the 13th September 1968, But before 
the hearing the Tribunal received a letter from the B . P . T . Railwaymen s Union that they 
did not want to pursue the above dispute as the workman concerned had ceased to be a 
member of their union and in view of the withdrawal of the union also the employers 
havo contended that the dispute is proved to be an individual dispute and the reference 
should be rejected with costs . 

9 . Thc lcarncd Counsel on behalf of the Bombay Port Trust has rclicd upon the ruling 
reported in 1961 II LI 436 Bombay Union of Journalists and others versus the Hindu 
Bombay and another and has argued that in view of the application by the emplovers 
for the production of documents and the non - production by the Union it should be held 
that the union had no mandate from the members of the union and the dispute being 
an individual dispute the reference should he rejected . 

10 It is clear from the order of reference and the copy of the failure report of conci 
liation sont along with it that the B . P . T . Railwaymen s Union had raised this dispute and 
Government had sent a copy of the reference orcler to the General Secretary of the B . P . T . 
Railwavmen s Union and this Tribunal will have jurisdiction if the dispute was an indus 
trial dispute . It is also clear from the ruling reported in 1961 II LLJ D 436 that for 
ascertaining whether an individual dispute has acauired the character of an industrial 
dispute the test is whether on the date of the reference the dispute was taken up and 
cupported by union of the workmen of the cmployer against whom the dispute is raised 
Isv the individual workman or by an anpreciable number of workmen . The circumstance 
that in spite of the Tribunal s order the union has not produced the documents which 
are in their possession indicates that there may be some truth in the contentions raised hy 
the Bombay Port Trust. However , I do not think it necrysary in this case to decide 
the question about the juiisdiction as the B . P . T . Railwavmen s union has informed this 
Tribunal that they dij not want to pursue the matter They have also remained ahcent 
ant it is clear that they do not want to prese the claim made by them in their writen 
statement and it shall have to be held that Shri Bahulal Nohar was not senior to Shr : 
Malavre und uc Shri Minovyy Wax senior the cniplovers had rromoted him to this post 
of fitter as per practice and rules. The promotion was in relation to the post which was 
an isolated one and it shall have to he presumed that the union did not want to mores 
its claim as there was no substance in the contention iaised and it shall have to be held

that Shri Babulal Nohar was not senior to Shri Malayya Ratnayya in relation to that 
post. 


- 


-- - - - 


- - - -

11. The workman Shri Babulal Nohar on whose behalf the dispute was raised was 
present at the time of the hearing. He has made it clear beforo the Tribunal that he 
has absolutely no dispute rogarding the promotion of Shri Malayya Ratnayya . He was 
at present holdings the management post of a boiler maker in the same scale and he did 
not want to make any demand . In view of thcsc circumstances it shall have to be held 
that the action of the managemot in treating Shri Malayya Ratnayya as senior to Shri 
Babulal llohar for the purpose of officiating or promotion was justified and Shri Bahulal 
Nohar is not entitlod to any relief. Hence my award accordingly .

AWARD 


By Order No. 28 / 85 /67 / 1.RIII, dated November 23, 1967 , the Government of India . 
in the Ministry of Labour, Employmont and Rehabilitation ( Department of Labour and 
Employment ), referrcd the following dispute , between the employers in relation to Messrs . 
Santosh Chandra Banerjee and Sons (Private ) Limited , and their workmen , to this tribunal 
for adjudication , namely : — 
" Whether the action of the management of Messrs. Santosh Chandra kuncrlce & 

Sons (Private ) Limited , Calcutta in terminating the services of Shri Sitaram 
Singh , Gearman with effect from the 10th November, 1965 was justified ? 
Whether the action of the management of Messrs. Santosh Chandra Baneriee 
and Sons (Private ) Limited , Calcutta in heeping Shri Sitaram Singh , Gearman 
under suspension from the 21st July , 1966 to the 7th December , 1966 after 
re-employment from the 7th April , 1966 was justified ? If not, to what relief 
is the workinan is entitled to either case ?" 


2 . Tho employer company did not appear in this reference , in spite of repeated nolices . 
Notice upon the employer company to file written stulement was served as far back as 
December 18 , 1967. They did not enter appearance nor d d they file any statement. 
Thereafter, in spite of notice they did not appear on August 6 , 1968, which was the date: 
for fixing a date of hearing of the reference . The date of hearing was communicated to 
the employer company but even then they did not appear on September 23 , 1968 , which 
was fixe i hearing. In these circumstances, I had to take up the reference for cr -parle 
hearing 


Sec . 3 (1 ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : NOVEMBER 9 , 1968 /KARTIKA 18, 1890 4973 


Ist 11 


3 . The workman , Shri Sitaram Singh , gave evidence in this case . He said that while 
in the cmploy of the company, he suffered physical injury , on July 20 , 1965, in his left 
leg . He further said that he was treated by the Medical Officer of the company at first 
and then got himself treated privately at the expense of the employer company. He relied 
on documents marked Exts, 1 and 2 in support of his case that he was ill and was unable 
to do his duty. Ho also said that he recovered and went to resume his duty on November 
10 , 1965. On that day , he way served with the following notice by the employer company 
(Ext. 3 ) : 
" This is to inform you that wbercas you left the place of duty without notice and 

remained untraced upto 2nd October, 1965, your scrvices will no longer 

be required by this company with immediate effect." 
To this letter the workmun submitted a rejoinder (Ext. 4 ), on Novembur 15 / 16 , 1963, 
the material portion of which is set out below : 
" That, you are aware of thc fact that I got injury on my left foot on 20th July , 

1965. In this respect X -Ray was done and facture was found . The physicion 

plastered ny injury and advised me to take 4 weeks rest on 27th July , 1965 . 
That, plaster was removed on 3rd September, 1965 , again X -Ray was done and the 

physiciun plastered the injury again on 8th September , 1965 and 2dvised me 

to takc rest for two months. 
That, the second plaster was removed on 9th November , 1965 and the physician 

advised me for X -Ray for check up . 
That, in view of the abovc facts it is clear that the question of leaving the place of 

duty without noticc and remaining untraced since 2nd October , 1965 does not 
arise at all. " 


At this stage, he deposed , he had to go home on rcccipt of a telegram about tiis father s 
death . Before his departure for home, he left an application for it month s leave with 
the employer company ( Ext. 5 ) . He admitted that he had to overstay and that from 
home he had to wish for cxtension of leave, all by registered post (Ext, 6 ) . He returned 
from huic, on February 5 , 1966 , and presented himself for duty but was kept unemploved , 
On April 7 , 1966 , however the employer company . condoned his conduct and set aside 
the penal order dated November 10, 1965, with a warning couched in the following letter : 

Reference to his Application dated 15th November, 1965 Sitarain Singh is hereby 

informed that this is io have been the last occasion he is condoned , thereby 

cancelling the notice of temination of his services, 
He is warned to the effect that any recurrence of negligence or lapse of duty will 

bring in his dismissal having no scope for further consideration . On this 

condition only he is directed to report to this office for duty. " 
Thus ended the first chapter of the misfortune of the workınan . 
4 . The next chapter , however, was soon to begin . In his evidence the workman said : 
" From July 19 , 1966 , Sri R , K . Bancrjee , Director of the Company, ordered me to 

attend the godown . At the godown I was given 10 duty to do . When 
I complained of this to tho management, I was suspended on July 20 , 1966 . 
The suspension order was withdrawn on December 7 , 1966 in the presence 
of my union ." 


The second chapter of the misfortune of the workman appears to have thus ended , 

5 . In this reference, there appears little justification for the order of termination , dated 
November 10 , 1965, and the order of keeping the workman suspended from July 21 , 1966 
to December 7 , 1966 because the employer company withdrew both the orders of their 
own accord . After withdrawal, the justification , if originally any , behind the orders were 
lost and in the absence of plcading and cvidence to the contrary . I have to proceed on 
the basis that thc orders were injustified . All that I am required to decide is that with 
the orders of termination and suspension being non- existent now , to what relief is the 
workman entitled in cither case . Since the order of termination of service was cancelled, 
according to Ext. 7 , for whatever rcason the samne may be , the workman becamc entitled 
to back wages , since termination till re -employment. Similarly , since the suspension order 
was withdrawn, the workman became entitled to his wages for the period of enforced 
idleness. In the absence of the cmployer company and in the absence of any material 
indicating the contrary position , I cannot resist this part of the claim inade by the workman , 
The question for my consideration , however , is the quantification of the wagcs duc or 
other monetary benefits . According to the cvidence given by the workman : 
" During November 1965 to December 7 , 1966 my pay was Rs. 129 per month . 

During November 20 , 1965 to April 7 , 1966 I only received Rs. 300 , owards 
my wages. I have no documentary cvidence to prove before this co rt that 


4974 THE GAZETTL OF INDIA : NOVEMBER 9, 1968 /KARTIKA 18 , 1890 


[PART II 


my pay Juring 1965 was Rs. 129 per inonth . In November and Deceinber 
1966 and January 1967 throc instalments of bonus were paid at the rate of 
Rs. 73 per instalment. I did not receive such bonuses . . . . . My pay during 
July 1966 to December 1966 was at the rate of Rs. 139 per month . During 
the period of suspension I did not receive anything. In May 1967, I was 

paid Rs. 100." 
This is the totality of cvidence on which I am required to quantify the monetary benefits 
available to the workman . 

6 . In the absence of documentary ovidence, I am unwilling to quantify , anymore than 
I do hereunder, the amount, on the oral evidence of the workman . In my opinion , the 
workman is entitled to his wages from 10th November , 1965 to April 6 , 1966 whatcvcr 
the amount may be . If he did not receive the l ull amount of bonus , he is also cntitled 
to receive the bonus instalments paid during that period as well . He is also entitled to 
his full wages , whatever that may be , for the period of his enforced idleness commencing 
from July 21 , 1966 to 7th December . 1966 He docs not claim any bonus for that period 
I make an award accordingly, 

( Sd .) B . N . BANERJEF , 

Presiding Officer.

AWARD 
The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour and Employment have by their 
Order No. 28 ( 32 ) / 66 -LRIV dated 6th July 1966 referred to this Tribunal the industrial 
dispute existing between the employers in relation to Messrs . Dharsi Moolji and their 
workman in respect of the matters specified in the following schedule : 

SCHEDULE 
" Whether the teimination of service of Shri Harichandra Narayan Chavan . 

Carpenter with effect from tho Ist December 1965 by the management of 
Messrs . Dharsi Moolji iy justified , If not to what relief is the workman 
cntitled ?" 


Subsequently the above schedule was amended in respect of the date and in line No. 2 
for " 185 December 1965" the words and figures " 1st January 1966 " were substituted . 

2 . The workman Shri Harichandra Narayan was in the employ of Messrs. Dharsi 
Moolji Boinbay and was working as a carpenter. On the 1st January 1966 after an 
absence of three months when he reported for work he was not allowed to resume work 
by the management and was stopped from work without notice and hence the Transport 


Sp . 3 ( li ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : NOVEMBER 9 , 1968/ KARTIKA 18 , 1890 4975 


and Dock Workers Union Bombay raised a dispute and contended that Shri Harichandra 
Narayan who was the permanent carpenter of the company had after obtaining sanction 
from the company proceeded on leave for three months from October 1965 to December 
1965 and when he icportod for duty on 1st January 1966 he was not allowed to resumo 
Work and the company had dismissed him illogally and with ulterior motives. Thoy bavo 
alleged that the dismissal amounted to victimisation and unfair labour practice . The 
company did not issue any charge - sheet against him nor held any enquiry according to 
tho principles of natural justice . They had referred the matter to the Conciliation Officer 
who discussed it with the parties from the 2nd February 1966 to 7th February 1966 
and on his failure report the Government has referred the dispute for adjudication . 

3 . The management has by their written statement denied the allegations mado by 
the union and have contended that Shri Harichandra Narayan was not attending work 
ropularly . In the month of July 1965 he had put in only 10 days work , in the month

again the parties negotiated the disputo and have prayed for a consent award .

Accountant and on behalf of the workman by his Advocate and the workman himself .

settled . In these circumstances I think it proper to accept the terms of settlement and I 
therefore make an award in terms of the Joint application of the parties containing the 
terms of settlement (Annexure A ) which shall form part of this award . 
No order as to costs.

8. 0 . 3927.- - In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 
1947 ), the Central Government horoby publishes the following award of the Industrial 
Tribunal, Calcutta in respect of complaint under Section 33A of the said Act Alled by 
Shri Sitaram Singh , Gearman employed by Messrs . Santosh Chandra Banorjeo and Sons 
( Private ) Limited , which was received by the Central Government on the 9th October, 
1968 . 
CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, CALCUTTA 

Misc . APPLICATION No . 2 OF 1968 

( UNDER Sec. 33A Of I. D . ACT ) 
PARTIES : 
Sri Sitaram Singh , Geanan , C / o Shipping Employees Union , 38 , Hemchandra 

Street, Calcutta - 23 . . . . . . . . . . . . Applicant. 


I s, 


M /s. Santosh Chandra Banerjee and Sons (P) Limited , 7 , Swallow Lane, Calcutta 1. 

. . . . . . Opp . Party.

Shri B . N . Banerjee . . . .. . . . Presiding Officer .

On behalf of Applicant - Shri H , L . Roy . 
On behalf of Opp . party — Absent. 

AWARD 
The applicant used to serve the opposite party , a company doing steyedoring business , 
as a Gearman . His services had been terminated earlier , on November 10 , 1965. That 
order the employer company themselves withdrew or cancelled later on . Thereafter , he 
was suspended and kept under suspension from July 21, 1966 to December 7 , 1966 . The 
suspension order was ultimately set aside by the employer Company and the applicant 
was re -employed only to be discharged with effect from February 1 , 1968 . 

2 . Over the first termination of service and the suspension order mentioned above , 
there was an industrial dispute raised and the dispute , in tho following form , was 
referred to this tribunal for adjudication, by Order No . 28 /85 /67 /LRIII dated Novombor 
23, 1967 : 
" Whether the action of the management of Messrs. Santosh Chandra Banerjos 

and Sons (Private ) Limited , Calcutta in terminating the services of Shri 
Sitaram Singh , Gearman with effect from the 10th November, 1965 was 
justified ? Whether the action of the management of Messrs. Santosh 
Chandra Banerjee and Sons (Private ) Limited , Calcutta , in keeping Shri 
Sitaram Singh , Gearnian under suspension from tho 21st July , 1966 to the 
7th December , 1966 after re - employment from the 7th April , 1966 was 
justified ? If not, to what relief is tho workman entitled to in either 

case ? " 
The above reference was registered as Roference No . 94 of 1967. While the abovo 
reference was pending (which was also pending at the time of hearing of this application ), 
there was thọ order of the discharge of the applicant made, by a letter dated December 
29, 1967 (Ext. 1 ) , the material portion of which roads : 
" Please take notice that as and from 1st February , 1968 your service is no longer 

required with us. You are requested to receive payment of your salary 
for the month of January , 1968 as early as possible . Please take notice as 

well that all the service Rules will be observed in your case." 
Against the order of discharge, the applicant made representation in writing, on January 
8 , 1968 , but to no effect. Unable to induce tho employer Company to withdraw tho 
order , the applicant filed a petition before this tribunal, under Section 33A of the Indus 
trial Disputes Act, praying for rescision of the order , inter alia , on the ground : 

" That the dispute relationg to Ref. No. 94 /67 was in relation to frequent dismissal, 

suspension , and termination of employment and denial of ducs viz., pay 

D . A ., Bonus, Interim relief and other benefits as to provident fund and 

gratulty and other. 
That before the case was taken up and without any ordorg or roferenço for approval 

of this Tribunal the employers have acted in excess and taken law in their 


SKC , 3(ii) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : NOVEMBER 9, 1968 /KARTIKA 18, 1890 4977 

- - - - - - - - - - - - - - - - 
own hands which is highly illegal and inoperative . The reprosentation of 
your humble petitioner to the opposito party was not taken in the 

consideration ( Annexure B )." 
3 . The employer company did not appear to show cause in spite of noticon. Notice 
W & sont to employer company , on March 28 , 1968 , calling upon them to show causo 
within two weeks of tho receipt of the notice . They received the notice , on April 2 , 1968 , 
but did not show cause . Notice was again sent to them asking them to appear before 
this tribunal, on August 20 , 1968 , or fixation of a date of hearing of the application . 
They did not appear , although the union of the applicant did so , on the 20th August, 
1968 . Information of the date fixed for hearing, namely September 5 , 1968, was sent 
to the employer company. On September 3 , 1968, tho case could not be heard , bocause 
tho ropresentative of the applicant was unprepared and in a bad shape and the employer 
company was also absent. The matter was, therefore , adjourned to September 23 , 1968 . 
The information of the adjournment was sont to the cmployer company by Registerod 
post. Even on the adjourned date of hearing , the employer company did not appear . 
In these circumstances, on September 23 , 1968 , I was compelled to proceed with tho 
case in the absence of the employer company . 

4 . It is no doubt true that the letter of termination of service of the applicant is 
couched in neutral language . It states that the services of the applicant would be no 
longer required from February 1 , 1968 . Taken by itself , it is virtually termination of 
service without blame-worthiness, and not discharge or dismissal by way of punishment. 

5. It is also true that the applicant was directly and solely concerned in the indus 
trial dispute which was the subject matter of Referenco No. 94 of 1967. Earlier 
termination of service and carlier suspension from servico of the very same workman 
was the subject matter of the industrial dispute pending in the Reference . The sub 
sequent order of termination of service will havo the effect of taking a good deal of 
sting out of the industrial dispute . Now , the circumstances of the case arc such that 
if the applicant be dealt or has been dealt with by the employer company under 
Section 33 (2 )( b ) of the Industrial Disputes Act, then the order could not be made without 
the approval of the tribunal, Mere termination of service , not falling under Section 
33 (2 )(b ), may not require approval but beyond mere words that services of the workman 
was being terminated , there is nothing to show that mere termination of service was 
being effected . In the instant case , the employer company avoided the tribunal and has 
not shown the reason for termination of service . There was no retrenchment going on 
and , on the inaterials before nie . I could not ascertain any justification for termination 
of service. On the other hand , the workman gave evidence and deposed : 
" The Secretary of the Company Nishapati Bhattacherjee became displeased with 

me because I did not want to serve him in his privato Affairs , without the 
written order from the employer . The present order of dismissal was made 
therefor during the pendency of an industrial dispute before this Tribunal. 
Ho possibly got annoyed with mo also because I had been making prayors 

for my dues." 
This evidence goes to show malafide intent behind the order of termination . If I believe 
in the workman , the Order of termination must be condemned as a penal order of dis 
charge without approval. The evidence is uncontradicted and I rely on the same. 

6 . In my opinion , the termination of service of the applicant was unjustified in the 
facts and circumstances of the casc and he is entitled to reinstatement in service with 
all back wages due to him from the dato of termination , 
I make an award accordingly . 

( Sd .) B . N . BANERJEE, 

Presiding Officor.

9 .O . 3929. - In pursuance of Section 17 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 
1947 ) , the Central Government hereby publishes the following award of the Arbitrator in 
tho Industrial dispute between Messrs . D . D . Contractor and Company Private Limited , 
Bombay and their workmen represented by the Transport and Dock Workers Union , Bom 
bay which was received by the Government on 25th October, 1968. 

BEFORE SHRI V . A . NAIK , ARBITRATOR , BOMBAY 
In the dispute 

BETWEEN 
M / 6. D . D . Contractor and Co ., Pvt . Ltd ., Bombay . 

AND 
Their workmen 19 represented by the Transport and Dock Workers Union, Bombay. 
In the matter of interpretation of Award in Reference CGIT -41 of 1964 in respect 

of dearness allowance .

Shri R . K . Dangle, Advocate , for the workmen . 

DECISION 


By an order dated 19th April 1968 the Government of India directed that the copy of 
tho agreement entered into between Messrs . D . D . Contractor and Co. Pvt. Ltd ., Bombay 
and the workmen , and received by the Government on 3rd April 1968 bc published , under 
sub- sec . ( 3 ) of Scc . 10A of the Industrial Disputes Act. Under the said agreement the 
dispute between the parties has been referred to me for arbitration. Tho specific matter in 
dispute has been mentioned in the following terms: 
" What is the correct interpretation of Para I- B of the consent Award of Shri Salim 

M . Merchant in Reference CGIT No. 41 of 1964 , in respect of payment of 
dcarness allowance payable on # monthly basis on the piece - rate monthly 
carnings of the registered workers employed by M / s . D . D . Contractor and 
Co ., Pvt. Ltd ., Bombay from 1st December 1964 as indicated in the scales 

of dearness allowance in thọ Award ." 
The parties have further agreed that the decision of the arbitrator shall be binding on 
then . The agrecment also provides that the arbitrator shall make his award within a 
period of 60 days or within such further time as is extended by mutual agreement between 
the parties in writing. On Sth August 1968 the parties entered into a further agrcement 
which states that the time for making the award is extended up to 30th September 1968. 

2 . There was a reference made to Shri Salim M . Merchant under Sec . 10 (2 ) of the 
Industriul Disputes Act for adjudication of the following demands : 
" ( 1 ) The following rates of wages should be paid to the workmen concerned for 

handling manganese ore or iron - orc : 
(a ) For the purpose of unloading manganose -ore or iron -ore from the wagons they 

should be paid at the rate of Rs. 2 per ton . 
( b ) They should be paid Rs. 3 per ton for loading the manganese -ore or iron - ore 

into the wagons and Rs. 4 per ton for loading lorries , 
(c They should be paid extra wages for the purposes of carrying the ore beyond 

a reasonable limit or for tho purposes of trimming or stacking ores at the 
plots. Similarly they should be paid at 11 times their normal rates it the 
OTR is directly transferred from wagon to wagon or from wagon to lorry ." 


Sec . 3 ( ii ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : NOVEMBER 9, 1968 /KARTIKA 18 , 1890 4979 
SAS 

... - - 
It is not necessary to refer to the other demands referred to Shri Salim M . Merchant, 

3 . A preliminary objection about the maintainability of the reference was raised on 
behalf of the cmployer . Shri Merchant however rejected the preliminary objection 
and held that the reference was valid . Thereafter an agreement was reached between 
the parties and Shri Merchant was invited to make his Award in terms of the said agree 
mont. Shri Merchant stated that he was satisfied that tho terms of the settloment pro 
vided the least that the employers can be called upon to pay to their workmen in respect 
of the demands under reference . He therefore made an award in terms of the agreement, 
Annexure B , I will now refer to the main terms of the consent award as regards dcarness 
allowance which is as follows: 
" Without prejudice to the contention of cither parties with regard to the applica 

bility or otherwise of the recommendations of the Cential Wage Board for 
Port and Dock Workers and although the piece - rated payments are payable 
to the workmen in question on a weekly basis or a fortnightly basis , as the 
case may be , D . A . will be payable on a monthly basis on the picce rated 
monthly earnings of the registered workmen . The D . A . will be payablo . 

with effect from 1st December 1964 as follows :

R ., 20

Explanation .. . Arrears arising out of payment of D .A , will be payable to the

As far as the present position regarding the recommendations of the Central 

Wage Board in respect of interim relief is concerned , the employers will 
not be under any obligation to pay to the workmen concerned any addi 
tional remuneration on account of the said recominendations of thc Centra 

Wage Board for the Port and Dock Workers , 
Although there is no demand for D . A , separately referred to adjudication , parties 

have no objection to divide the total cmoluments into two categories viz . 
piece - rate and D . A . as long as such total emolumonts does not cxceed the 

demand regarding wages as referred . 
4 . The parties were called upon to file their statements . M / s D . D . Contractor in 
its statement stated that the dearness allowance payable to the workmen of the company 
in terms of the said award is payable on a monthly basis depending upon the piece rate 
earnings of the workmen . They further stated that the correct interpretation of the 
award so far as dearness allowance was concerned would be that payment of dearness 
allowance is not only linked to the piece rate carnings of the workmen but also on his 
attondance during the month . They assert that not linking up the payment of deerness 
allowance which is payable on a monthly basis to the attendance of the workmen would 
load to absurd results which could not have been intended by the parties and was cer 
trinly not irìtended by the company . 

S . Tho workmen in the first instance did not put in their written statement. The 
matter was argued by Shri Dangle on behalf of the workmen but since the arguments 
could not be completed the case was adjourned to another date . On the adjourned date 
that is on 3rd August 1968 Shri Dangle submitted the written statement signed by Shri 
S . R . Kulkarni, the Secretary of the Union. In this written statement the Union has 
raised a number of questions which fall beyond tho purview of this reference. The 
Union has asked for the production of certain records maintained by the employer uuder 
the previous Memorandum of Settlement relating to the attendance of the workers at the 
place of work for the period 1st December 1964 to September 1967. It is further stated 
that the nature of work required to be done by the workmen is such that on certain 
occasions they are required to work more than one shift in 24 hours and they are paid 
piece rate wages every fortnight, and even thon dearness allowance is paid at the end 
of the month on the basis of total picce rate earnings of the workmen in a given month . 
These questions of fact have been raised practically after tho arguments were over. I 
am not concerned with the question about the prcvailing practice . I do not therefore 
think it necessary to call for any records. Again no affidavit has been produced to show 
the exact nature of work or to prove the allegations that the workmen concerned are 
required to work more than one shift in 24 hours. Since the piece rate is fixed for the 
out-put, the question of time or the number of shifts is not of much importance. The 
only matter that is referred to me relates to the interpretation of tho language used in 
tho consent Award of Shri Salim M . Merchant in respect of payment of dearness allowance . 
I will now proceed to deal with thọ points urged in this case . 


- 4980 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : NOVEMBER 9, 1968 /KARTIKA 18. 1890 (PART II — 


- - - 


6 . There was no specific demand for dearness allowance. The only demnand made 
was in respect of piece rate wages for loading and unloading. It was demanded that 
for the purpose of unloading manganese -ore or iron -ore from wagons the workinen 
should be paid at the rate of Rs. 2 per ton , and they should be paid Rs. 3 per ton for 
loading the manganese -ore or iron - ore into the wagons and Rs. 4 per ton for loading 
lorries . The demand does not refer to the number of workmen but the agreement 
refors to the rate per ton per gang . I was told that although the words per gang have 
not been used in the demand itşelf these words were understood between the parties and 
there was no controversy on that point. The demand therefore must be understood to 
refer to the rate per gang per ton . In the agreement on the basis of which an Award 
was made the total emoluments was divided into two parts VIZ., Piece ratc payment 
and dearness allowance. The last para of the explanation reads thus: 


" Although there is no demand for D . A . scparately referred to adjudication , parties 

have no objection to divide the total emoluments into two categories viz . 
Piece - rate and D . A . as long as such total emoluments do not exceed the 

demand regarding wages as referred " . 
7 . The dispute now referred is in respect of the terms employed in respect of dearness 
allowance . The material term runs: 

. . . . D . A . will be payable on a monthly basis on the piece iated monthly carn 

ings of the registered workmen . 
Shri Phadke emphasised the expression dearness allowance will be payable on the 
monthly basis on the piece rated monthly earnings and argued that the workmen must 
work for 26 days in a month in order to earn any dcarness allowance at all . According 
to him the rates of dcarness allowance have been fixed on the basis that the workmen work 
for the whole month that is 26 days. On the other hand Shri Dangle emphasised the use 
of the words " Rs. 20 should be paid as dearness allowance for picce rated earnings bolow 
Rs. 109 " . According to Shri Dangle this means that howsoever meagre the earnings of 
the workmen , the dearaçss allowance is Rs. 20 so long as the earnings are below Rs. 109. 
Shri Phadke contended that the use of the words below Ry. 109 is semantically inaccu 
rate . The proper words should have becn " up to Rs. 109 " . He pointed out that even 
zero is below Rs. 109. He contended that it would be absured that a workman who has 
worked for a day and has earned a sumn of Rs. 2 for instance is entitlel to an amount of 
Rs. 20 as dearness allowance . According to him the very concept of dearness allowance 
payment is to supplement basic wages , to off set or neutralise the effect of the rise in 
prices. There is considerable substance in this argument. The interpretation sought to be 
put by Shri Dangle would lead to absurd results . It may happen that a workman hus 
worked with a particular contractor for a single day and has earned a small sum of Re. I 
or Rs. 2 ; for the remaining days he may have worked for a number of other contractors 
for short periods in such a case the concerned workman will he entitled to dearness 
allowance at the rate of Rs. 20 from each of the contractors, Shri Dangle argued that a 
worker has to report himself every morning for duty to the contractor and ask whether 
there is enough work for the day . There is no evidence in support of the statement nor is 
it open to me to take that aspect into account in this reference , but assuming for a 
moment that this is the practice , it will make no difference . As soon as he comes to know 
from one contractor that there is no work for the day he will go to another contractor 
and work for him . The merc fact that he has to report for work to one contractor does 
not mean that he has worked for that contractor for that day . If there is work with 
another contractor the workman is free to go and carry out that worh . The workmen 
working with the contractor do not appear to be permanent workmen . They are a 
fluctuating body . Therefore they may go for work to any contractor. 

8 . It will be seen that the first part of the agreement states that although the piece 
rated payments are payable to the workmen on a weekly basis or a fortnightly basis as 
the case may be, the dearness allowance will be payable on 7 monthly basis on the piece 
rated monthly earnings of the registered workmen . This means that the wage periods for 
piece rates will continue to be either weekly or fortnightly . as the case may be , but for 
dearness allowance payment tho wage - period will always be by the month . The agreement 
further provides in the last para that although there is no demand for dcarness allowance 
separately referred to adjudication , the parties have no objection to divide the total cmolu 
ments into two categories piece rate and D . A . This is subject to the over -riding condition 
that " the total amoluments should not exceed the demand regarding wages as referred " . 
For A true interpretation of the agrcement it is necessary to look to the intention of the 
parties . Even though no demand was made by the cmployees for dearness allowance , the 
employer conceded a slub system of dearness allowance linked to the piece rates . This 
shows that the dearness allowance was given as an incentive yo that those who work more , 
carn more by regular attendance . Since the slab system of dearness is linked to piece 
rates tho factors which are implicit when computing the dearnegs allowance are the daily 
carnings of the employees calculated on the piece rates which very according to the naturo 


SEC: 3 ( ii )] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : NOVEMBER 9 , 1968 /KARTIKA 18, 1890 4987 
- - - - - - - - - - - 

= - - - - - - - - = - - - - 
of the operations and the tonnage ; and for the purpose of ascertaining the slab , the monthly 
carnings of the employees which in turn is the sum total of their daily earning for tho 
number of days worked in a particular month . Thus the tonnage, the nature of the 
operations, the picce rates , the number of days worked in a month and the total monthl, 
eamings arc co -related in the slab system of dearness allowance prescribed under the 
urcement. The expression " payable on a monthly basis" could not mean as was urged 
by Shri Phadke that a workman must work for all working days , that is 26 days in a 
month . The expression "monthly basis " has a reference to the mode of calculation and 
the dearness allowance is to be calculated on the basis of the piece rate monthly earnings 
If Shri Phadke s argument is accepted it would mean that even though the workman has 
worked for 25 days in a month , that is one day less and earned Rs, 109 or more he would 
not get a single pie by way of deurness allowance . The attendance of the workman will 
play an important part in calculating the dearness allowance the workman has earned . 
For example if he attends to his dutics for 13 days in a month and cams Rs. 109 ho 
would be entitled to Rs. 20 as urged by Shri Dengle , he would be entitled to only a pro 
portionate payment that is to say in proportion to the number of days he has worked ; that 
means that he will get Rs. 10) as dearncys allowance . According to Shri Phadke s argument 
he would get nothing. That obviously would lead to not only hardship but also an anomally , 
Suppose again a worker has achieved 1 1 / 2 times or double the tonnage achieved by 
another during the 13 days and the picce rated monthly earnings come to Rs. 150 then he 
would be cntitled lot to Rs. 30 as urged by Shri Dengle but he would be entitled to half 
of Rs. 30) because he has worked for 13 days during the month . There is another difficulty 
in accepting the argument of Shri Phadke and it is this. Supposing a worker is ready to 
work for all the 26 days in a month but the contractor is not in a position to supply him 
work for all the days but can give him work only for a few days. The logical corrolary 
of Shri Phadke s argument will be that even in that cvent the worker would not get any 
dearness allowance . This is obviously unfair . If Shri Dengle s argument is accepted 
namely that a worker is entitled of Rs. 20 dearness allowance whatever his carnings and 
whatever the number of days hc has worked it would mean that he will get dearness 
allowance without carning any wages . That will be opposed to the fundamental concept. 
of dcarness allownce . Shri Danglc s argument therefore cannot be accepted . We can 
take another illu -tration , a worker has worked for 25 days and on the 26th day he falls 
ill, according to Shri Phadke s contention he will not be entitled of any dearness allowance 
at all. It will thus be scen that the lines of arguments adopted by the respective counsel 
Arc lhe extrcme lines and Icad to illogical and anomalous consequences . The only correct 
interpretation of the terms of the award therefore is to hold that u worker is entitled to 
derrness allowance according to the tablc or schedule prescribed in the consent Award in 
Proportion to the number of days he has worked and I interpret the award accordingly . 

Sd / - V . A . NAIK , 

Arbitrator. 
Bombu . the 23rd September 1968.

Government Industrial Tribunal, Calcutta , in the industrial Dispute between the cmployers 
in relation to the East Chora Colliery, Post Office Bahula , District Burdwan and their 
workmon , which was received by the Central Government on the 18th October , 1968 . 


CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL , CALCUTTA 

REFERENCF No . 13 OF 1968 


PARTIES : 
Employers in relation to the East Chora Colliery . 

AND

Presiding Officer 
APPEARANCES - 
On behalf of Employers. Shri K . C . Nandkeolyur , 

Dy. Chicf Personnel Officer. 
On behall of Workers. - Shri Robin Chatterjee , Vice -President, 

Colliery Mazdoor Sabha , 
STATE : WEST BI.NGAL. 

INDUSTRY : Coal Mincs . 

AWARD 
By Order No . 6 /94 /67-LRII , dated March 2 , 1968, the Government of Indid , in the 
Ministry of Labour , Employment and Rehabilitation ( Department of Labour 400 
Empiovinent), referred the following dispute between the employers in iclation to the Eust 
Chora Colliery and their workmen , to this tribunal, for adjudication , namely : 
" Whether the management of East Chora Colliery , Post Office Bahula , District 

Burdwan was justified in changing the trado of Shri Nandalal Mahato 
On -setter Banksmasi 10 a Trammer ? If not, to what relief is the workman 
entitled ? " 


2 . The cause of the workman concerned was espoused by the Colliery Mazdsor Sabha , 
1 tiade union . The Mazdoor Sabha filed a written statement. The employer company 
did not filc any written statement. At the hearing of the reference Mr. Robin Chatterjee , 
Vice- President of the Colliery Mazdoor Sabha, appeared for the workman . Mr. K . C . 
Nandkcolyar, Deputy Chief Personnel Officer , appcared for the cmployer company. Partics 
did not examine any witness. Certain documents produced on behalf of the workman , 
by the trade union , were marked , by consent, formal proof being dispensed with 

3 . It is not disputed that the workman concerned used to work as an On -setter at the 
matcrial time. On July 13 , 1967, the following notice ( Ext. A2) was sent to the workman 
by the Manager of the employer company : 
" In accordance with section 9A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, your services 

are hereby changed as U . G . Trammer with effect from 6th August, 1967. 
Therefore, you are requested to report your duty accordingly ." 
Thereafter, on August 3 , 1967 , the manager of the employer company wrote the following 
further letter to the workman (Ext. A1) : 
" As per our letter vide No. LW / ( 7 ) / 172 /67 dated 15th July , 1967 Form E (Rulo 

34 ) of Industrial Disputes ( ? ) of 1947 and in accordance with Section 9A 
of Industrial D sputes Act, 1947 your services are hereby changed as U / G 

Trammer with effect from 6th August, 1967. 
Therefore , you are requested to join your duty accordingly." 


Notices of this change appear to have been given to the Chief Labour Commissioner (C ), 
Regional Labour Commissioner ( C ) , Asansol, Assistant Labour Commissioner ( C ), Ranigan ), 
The General Secretary , Colliery Mazdoor Congress (HMS), and in different notice boards 
of the company s colliery office ( vide Ext. A3 ) . The change of designation or work was 


Sec . 3 ( i ) ] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : NOVEMBER 9 , 1968/ KARTIKA 18 , 1890 4983 
- - - 

— - - - - - - - 
inacceptable to the workman concerned and the reason why it was so was stated in 
paragraph 6 of the written statement, which I set out below : 
" The nature of work of Trammer as well as the scale of pay of Trammer being 

different from that of the work of Banksman On - setter, Shri Mabato regretted 

his inability to accept the terms. " 
4 . Mr. Robin Chatterjee , who appeared for the union , raised a threefold contention 
for my consideration . He contended , in the first place, that on the pretext of change of 
designation or work , the workman concerned was rcally reduced in rank . Ile contended , 
in the next place , that the pay and emoluments of a trammer was less than the pay and 
emoluments of an On - sotter and by reason of the order the workman concerned was 
suffering financial loss . He argued lastly that the procedure adopted in effecting the change 
was illegal and the workman was being really victimised by thc cmployer company. 

5 . Mr. K . C . Nandkęolyar , who appeared on behalf of the employer company, made 
two broad concessions in favour of the workman . The first concession made by him was 
that the workman concerned will be treated as time rated trammer and be placed in 
Category V to which all Bankşmcn and On - sctters are placed , under paragraph 153 of 
Labour Appellate Tribunal ( Collicry Disputes) Award of the year 1957 . The second 
concession made by him was that the workman concerned will be drawing the same pay 
and cinojuments as he was drawing as an On -setter , even though he may be working as a 
trammer. He also made it clcar that if, in tho meantime, anything less by way of pay and 
emoluments has been paid to the workman , the samo would be made up by the employer 
company . 

6 . Now , regard being had to the concessions made by Mr. Nandkcolyar, it cannot be 
said that the workman concerned has any further substantial gricvance . The letter of change 
(Ext. A2) did not indicate that he was being placed in a reduced rank. It appears from 
paragraphs 150 and 153 of the Labour Appellate Tribunal Award in Colliery Disputes : 
" 150 . * * * we think that the proper place of the piece - rated Trummer whether 

working underground or on the surface would be in Category V . We would , 
therefore , dircct that the existing rates of the piece- rated Trammçr should 

ho revised and raised * * * . 
153. Since we have now placed the time-rated Trammers in Category IV it will be 

necessary to upgrade banksmen and On -setters (item 93 and 94 ) who shall 
be put in Category V , and * * * " . 


Mr. Robin Chatterjee contended that the order of change does not indicate that the work 
toan concerned will be placed in the rank of time-ratod trammers. Mr. Chatterjee is no 
doubt right in this contention . But because of the concession made by Mr. Nandkeolyar, 
thc criticism loses its force . I make it clear , by this award , that by the change the workman 
concerned shall be deemed to have been placed in Category V and shall be drawing the 
sanle pay and emoluments as he was cntitled to do as an On -setter . I further make it 
clear that if in the meantime the workman has been paid anything less, the same would 
he made up by the employer company . 


7 . This disposes of the first and second contention of Mr. Chatterjee that i demotiott 
took place , which made the workman suffer financially . 


8 . I now turn to the last contention of Mr. Chatterjee that the procedure adopted by 
the cmployer company was illegal and the change was made only to victimise the concern 
a workman . There is no evidence that the procedure Under Section 9A of the Industrial 
Disputes Act was not followed . There is also no cvidence of any victimisation . In the 
absence of any evidence . I cannot uphold this branch of argument of Mr. Chatterjee . 


9 . In the result, my award is that the management of East Chora Colliery was justifia 
it changing the trade of Nandalal Mahato from an On -setter to a trammer. The effect of 
this change , however , will not make the workman concerned lose his grude or cause any 
roduction in his pay or emolument. He shall remain in Grade V as time- rated trammer , 
ay envisaged in the Labour Appellate Tribunal (Colliery Disputes) Award and shall continue 
to draw the same pay and emoluments as he used to do as an On - setter , 
This is my award . 

( Sd.) B . N . BANERJEE , Presiding Officer . 
Dated , October 14 , 1968.

Employment & Rehabilitation Department of Labour & Employment), Government of 
India , referred the following matter of dispute as stated in tho schedulo to the order of 
reference to this Tribunal, for adjudication : - - 

Matter of Dispute 
Whether the intended closure of one shift of Umaria Colliery with effect from 18t 

August, 1967 by the management of Umaria Colliery of Messrs Rewa Coal 
Fields Limited , Post Office Dhanpuri, and asking the workers to opt for 
pension or gratuity and also proposing transfer of surplus workers from 
Umaria Colliery to other collieries without following the procedures as laid 
down in Chapter V A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 is justified ? If 

not to what relief are the workmçn entitled ? 
After issue of usual potices, parties filed written statements followed by rejoinders and 
on tho basis of pleadings certain additional issues were also framed on Sth February , 1968 .

which in fact has now been rendered . It appears that not only the shift in question , but 
the working of the entire collicry has been closed by the employers in consequence of

by the closure have been given employment at Burbar, another collicry of the employers , 
with continuity of service and without any adverse affect, the Union did not press the 
dispute . The terms of settlement are reproduced in the adnexure . Accepting the terms, the 
consent awant is hereby recorded in terms of compromise settlement.

The Central Government being of the opinion that an industrial disputc cxists betwech 
the employers in relation to the Bhowra Colliery , P . O . Bhowra , District Dhanbad and 
their workmen , by its order No. 2 /68 / 66 -LRIC dated the 6th June, 1966 , referred to the 
Central Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad under Section 10 ( 1 ) ( d ) of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 1947 for lidjudication the dispute in respect of the matters specificd in the Schedulo 
annexed thereto . The Scheduic is cxtracted below : 

SCHEDULE 
" Whether the dismissal of Shri Sheonath Lodh and Sobhay Lodh Miners with effect 

from 18th January , 1966 and suspension of Hublal Passi, Jailal Passi, 
Raindayal Passi, Pancham Passi, and Sarjoo Passi, miners , from 29th December , 
1965 to 15th January , 1966 , by the management of Bhowra Colliery was 

justified ? 
If not to what relief arc they cntitled ? 
2 . The Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Dhanbad registered the reference as 
Reference No. 114 of 1966 on its file . By Central Government s Order No . 8 / 25 / 66 -1,RII 
dated the 8th May , 1967, the reference was transferred to the Central Government Industrial 
Tribunal (No. 2 ) at Dhanbad where it was numbered as Reference No . 160 of 1967. By 
subscquent order No. 8 / 72 /68 - 1- RII dated the 17th August , 1968, the Central Government 
transferred the dispute to this Tribunal. This Tribunal registered the reference as Rcfc 
rence No . 31 of 1968, on its file . 

3 . The workmen represented by Shri Ram Mitra , Secretary , Bihar Koyala Mazdoor 
Sabha , P . O . Bhowra , District Dhanbad filed written statement on 20th July , 1966 . They 
stated therein that during the week ending 4th December, 1965 , 11th December, 1965 
land 18th December, 1965 weekly curning fell short than the minimum guaranteed wages 
us per ( oal Award in respect of Shconath Lodh , Shobhay Lodh and few others. These 
miners refused to accept lower wages offered to them by the manageinent and subinitted 
severai petitions before the nanagement for the payment of the minimum guaranteed 
wages . But the management refused to redress grievance of the workers . 

4 . On 28th December, 1965 in the second shift while the concerned v. orkinen were 
engaged in their duties , Bancham Passi way assaulted with the result that Shri Pancham 
Passi received serious injuries on his head. Pancham Passi wanted to get treatment from 
the Colliery Hospital for which he was legally enttled but the manarement delibcratcly 
refused to give him medical treatment. On refusal Shri Pancham Passi went to the 
State Dispensary at Jhuria for his treatment. On 29th December, 1965 in the morning 
Pancham Passi, Ramdayul PASS , Saijoo , Sheonath Lodh , Sobhay Lodh , Hublal and Jai 
Lal (Affected workmen ) submitted a joint petition to the Manager stating the facts of

-

assault and demanded suitablc action against the culprits. Their contention is that after 
receiving th : joint petition instead of taking action against the real culprits the nuagumont 
issued charge slicets to the concerned workmen and suspended them from 29th December , 
1965 pending the enquiry. 

5. After receiving the charge sheets the concerned workmen submitted their reply to 
the charge sheets denying the charge and also stated the true facts and demanded action 
agairy the real culprits. But the management gave protection to the real culprits and 
proceeded agaiist the concerned workmen , Their case is that no enquiry was held and 
no fair chance was given to them to defend themselves and to produce their witnesses 
and documents. The concerned workmn submitted joint petition before the management 
on 7th January , 1966 and demanded I fair enquiry but no reply was given to them . Sub 
sequently the management allowed Hublal Passi, Jallal Passi, Ramdayal Passi, Pancham 
Passi and Sarjou Passi to resume their work with effect from 15th January , 1966 and

Jailal Passi, Ramdayal Passi, Sarjoo Pass and Shconath I odh challenged to

Shri S . K . Chhibba, overman was recorded and the recording of the statement was over 
at 1 P . M . and the cnquiry was adjourned and fixed on the following day ie 4th January 
1966 at 9 A M . On 4th January 1966 thc workmen concerned did not turn up for the 
enquiry . Thereafter the enquiry was adjiurned on Stth January 1966 at 4 P . M . Even 
on 5th January 1966 the workmen concerned did not attend the enquiry . The coquiry 
was then adjourncd and it was fixed on the 6th January , 1966 at 4 - 30 P . M . On that day 
though the workoren concerned were present, they left the place of enquiry without any 
reason and Shconath I odh told the Enquiring Officer that they would not attend the enquiry 
and that the Enquiring Oficer could do whatever he liked. The enquiry thereafter pro 
ceeded ex -parle . In the absence of the workmen concerned the statements of the witnesses 
on behalf of the management were recorded . The management was of the view that 
Shconath Lodh ankl Shobay Lodh were the persons who were mainly and directly respon 
sible for th : asult and that involvement of other workers concerned was of lesser degree . 
The Enquiring Officer therefore recommended dismissal of Sheonath Lodh and Shobay 
Lodh and suspension of the remaining five workmen concerned. The case of the manage 
ment is that they took all possible steps to hold proper enquiry but the workmçn concerned 
intention lly avoided the enquiry and therefore the management had to proceed with the 
enquiry (. - parte . 

8 . The concerned worknien denied the charge. Ext. M .8 is their reply to the charge 
shect. Their case is that on 28th December 1965 in the second shift at about 10 - 30) P M . 
Pancham Passi and Ramdayal were assaulted and that Pancham Passi received serious in 
juries on his heid . They contend that the management has not taken any action against 
the real culprits and that the management took side of the offenders and as such served 
charge sheets to the workmen concerned . 

9 . It appears that the management did not consider explanation of the concerned work 
dien to the charge sheet, to be satisfactory and decided to enquire into the matter . The 
enquiry was ordered to be held on 3rd January 1966 at 9 A . M . On that date the con 
cernet wormen were present. The workers also filed a petition to the effect that they 
muy he represented by the worknian Lakhan Singh in the enquiry . On 3rd January 1966 
Shri S . K . Chh bha , Overman on bchalf of the management was examined and cross - CX 
amined . Only one witness was examined on that date and the enquiry was adjourned to
